     - B -> H -> I -> J -> P -> D -> J -> M
     - B -> H -> I -> J -> M

     - B -> G -> F -> K -> X -> H -> I -> J -> M
     - B -> G -> F -> K -> X -> H -> E -> L -> M

   - Nodes connected directly to M: E, L, I, D (but they can reroute)
   - Nodes connected directly to B: H, C, G
   - Removing H blocks the direct path B-H-E-L-M.
   - However, further analysis shows that removing just H leaves multiple alternative paths. For example, B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> P -> J -> M.

   - Nodes P, J, and I could be intermediate stops in many alternate paths but removing them alone wouldn’t suffice.
   - Thus, H and E must be strategic together to cover redundant routes.

     - B -> [H or E] would fail short.
     - B -> C without both wouldn’t sever other redundant paths effectively.

6. **Verification**:

   - Removing H and E will ensure all paths are severed.

Thus, the shortest path from F to J is:
F → E → B → H → I → P → J.

To determine the minimum number of edges to remove in order to completely disconnect the two black nodes (F and D) while ensuring they remain intact, we analyze all possible paths between them and identify the critical edges that must be cut.

**Step-by-Step Analysis:**

1. **Identify all paths between F and D:**
    - Path 1: F → G → K → H → I → E → M → J → D
    - Path 2: F → G → K → X → H → I → E → B → N → O → C → B → P → J → D
    - Path 3: F → G → K → H → P → E → M → J → D
    - Path 4: F → G → K → X → H → P → E → M → J → D

2. **Determine critical edges:**
    - From Path 1: Remove (G, K) to cut off the direct connection from F to the central part of the graph.
    - From Path 2: After removing (G, K), another path can still exist through (X, H) or (H, I). We need to remove an edge along either of these paths.
    - Removing (H, I) cuts off multiple paths (2 and 4) passing through the central part of the graph.
    - From Path 3: Removing (K, H) ensures that any path passing through K and H to J and then D is cut off.
    - From Path 4: Removing either (X, H) or (K, X) ensures the remainder of the path through X to H is cut off.

3. **Optimizing the cut set:**
    - Ensuring full disconnection requires cutting shared edges that reduce redundant paths.
    - Verify that removing (G, K) and (H, I) is sufficient to break all identified paths: Since none of the remaining edges form another complete path, this is minimal.

4. **Final Verification:**
    - Cutting (G, K) eliminates paths through the local connection of F.
    - Cutting (H, I) eliminates paths through H and I ensuring no other re-routing of paths from the central graph to D.

**Final Answer:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(G, K), (H, I)]

--- Model Response ---

The black nodes are **E** and **I**.

### Paths from E to I:
1. **Direct vertical path through L**
2. **Path through F, H, B, N, C, P**
3. **Path through M, J, P**

### Paths from I to E:
1. **Direct vertical path through P, C, N, B, H, F**
2. **Path through P, J, M**

Now, we need to find the nodes whose removal will disconnect E and I.

### Potential Cut Sets:
- **Removing L**: Eliminates the direct vertical path from E to I.
- **Removing F**: Breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **Removing H**: Breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **Removing B**: Breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **Removing N**: Breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **Removing C**: Breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **Removing M**: Breaks the path through M, J, P.
- **Removing J**: Breaks the path through M, J, P.

However, we need the minimum number of nodes. Let's identify the smallest set:

- **L**: Removing L eliminates one direct path.
- **C or N**: Removing either C or N breaks the path through F, H, B, N, C, P.
- **M or J**: Removing either M or J breaks the path through M, J, P.

By removing two nodes, **L and C**, we ensure that all paths connecting E and I are broken:
- **L** breaks the direct vertical path.
- **C** breaks the horizontal path through F, H, B, N, C, P.

### Verification:
1. **L and C removed**:
  - Paths through F, H, B, N eliminated.
  - Direct vertical path through L eliminated.
  - Any remaining path (like M-J) is not connected after removing C (since P is connected to both sides but C acts as a bridge).

Thus, the minimal cut set requires removing two nodes.

**Final Answer:** Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [L, C].
